# The second banquet; Haman hanged (Esth 7)

> **Status:** Preliminary research · research status `publishable` · revision 1 · last reviewed: not reviewed
> Programmatic validation is not historical verification. Classifications are machine-proposed unless marked as owner-reviewed.

## Biblical references

- Esther 7:1–10 (narration)

## Biblical account

At the second banquet the king again offered Esther up to half the kingdom; she asked for her life and her people's, who had been sold to be destroyed; when he asked who had done this, she named Haman. The king went into the garden in wrath; Haman stayed to beg Esther and was found fallen on her couch; Harbonah told of the gallows fifty cubits high made for Mordecai, and the king ordered Haman hanged on it.

## Historical setting

**Period:** Spring 474 BC

The day after the first banquet. [1]

### Rulers

- Xerxes I: King of Persia (Ahasuerus) [1]

**Chronological statements:**

- “they hurried to bring Haman to the banquet” (Esther 6:14–7:1) → `days_later` The sleepless king; Mordecai honoured by Haman (Esth 6)

## Geography

- Susa (Shushan) (Susa; confidence: certain)
  - Susa (Pleiades 912936): certain [2]

## Claims and evidence

### Haman was executed after Esther exposed his plot.

**Classification:** No independent corroboration found (machine proposed) · layer: evidence

Known only from Esther and its retellings (Josephus follows the biblical story); no Persian or Greek source read mentions it.

- *Context (indirect):* Haman was executed after Esther exposed his plot. (Esth.7.6-Esth.7.10) [3]

## Related passages and events

- this event → **thematic parallel** → Esther before the king; the first banquet; Haman's gallows (Esth 5) (high): The gallows prepared in 5:14.
- this event → **followed by** → Mordecai's counter-decree of 23 Sivan (Esth 8; June 474 BC) (high): Haman's house and office given away.

## Commentary (interpretation, not historical evidence)

- Guzik notes the irony that the king had himself authorised the plot he now condemns. [3]
